\hline\cr\end{array})=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{S}_{s}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr b&\mathbf{S}_{s-\ell+1}&\cdots&\mathbf{S}_{s-1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

where
b=\mathbf{S}_{s-\ell}
or
b=u_{1}
.

Proof.

In the process of performing the bumping algorithm for the right-hand side,
we find

\col(\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr b&\mathbf{S_{s-\ell+1}}&\cdots&\mathbf{S_{s-1}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array})\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{S_{s}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}=\col(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr b\\
\hline\cr\end{array})\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{S_{s-\ell+1}}&\cdots&\mathbf{S}_{s-1}&\mathbf{S}_{s}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

as required.
∎

Using Lemmas
A.1
,
A.2
and
A.3
,
we can easily show that

T_{\ell}\left(u_{1}^{\otimes c}\otimes\bigotimes_{j=0}^{s-1}\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{S}_{s-j}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}\right)=u_{1}^{\otimes c+\min\{s,\ell\}}\otimes\bigotimes_{j=0}^{s-1}\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{S}_{s-j}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Using Lemma
3.6
,
this is sufficient to prove Theorem
4.8
for general states.

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
4.14

B.1.
Reductions

Consider
\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(m|n)
with height
r
tableaux, and assume
m\geq r+2
.
Set,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{m}$}\\
\hline\cr\vdots\\
\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{m-r+3}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array},

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{m-r+2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array},

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{m-r+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array},

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{m-r}\\
\hline\cr\end{array},

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{m-r-1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}.

Note:
\mathbf{3}<\mathbf{2}<\mathbf{1}<\mathbf{0}
.

We employ the following notation:

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr x^{a}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}=\underbrace{\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr x&x&\cdots&x\\
\hline\cr\end{array}}_{a}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr(x_{j})_{j=1}^{a}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{a}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}.

To prove Theorem
4.14
in the case where
m\geq r+2
, it suffices to prove the following proposition:

Proposition B.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a genuine highest weight state in the
U_{1}(\mathfrak{gl}(r))\oplus U_{q}(\mathfrak{gl}(m-r|m))
-crystal
\mathcal{B}_{\overline{0},\overline{m-r}}
. That is,

(B.1)

\mathfrak{p}=u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{2}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{w}_{1}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{y}_{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{j}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{2}&\text{if }j\leq L\\
\mathbf{3}&\text{if }j>L\end{cases},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{y}_{j}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{0}&\text{if }j\leq M\\
\mathbf{1}&\text{if }j>M\end{cases},

for some non-negative integers
L,M\leq\mathfrak{d}_{2}
.
If
\mathfrak{c}_{2}+L\geq\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M
and
t\in\mathbb{Z}
is sufficiently large,
then

(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t}(\mathfrak{p})=u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{3}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{4}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{w}_{1}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{y}_{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

with phase shift
\delta=2\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L-M
.

Indeed, by Lemma
3.6
, certain crystal operators
commute with the time evolution operator
T_{\ell}
.
Since any state can be reached by successively applying crystal operators to a genuine highest weight state
[
BKK00
]
,
it is sufficient to prove Theorem
4.14
for genuine highest weight states of
\mathcal{B}_{\overline{0},\overline{m-r}}
.

All genuine highest weight states are given by
B.1
.
Indeed,
the vacuum elements are invariant under the crystal operators of
\mathcal{B}_{\overline{0},\overline{m-r}}
,
so can be safely be ignored.
Additionally,
given the assumption
k=r
in Theorem
4.14
,
we can treat the bottom row and the other rows as two seperate crystals,
(which are a
U_{q}(\mathfrak{gl}(r))
-crystal and a
U_{q}(\mathfrak{gl}(m-r|n))
-crystal respectively).
Finally, due to the semistandard assumption of each soliton,
the bottom row is isomorphic to the
U_{q}(\mathfrak{gl}(r))
-crystal

B^{1,\mathfrak{d}_{1}}\otimes B^{1,\mathfrak{d}_{2}}

and the other rows are isomorphic to the
U_{q}(\mathfrak{gl}(m-r|n))
-crystal

B^{(r-1),\mathfrak{d}_{1}}\otimes B^{(r-1),\mathfrak{d}_{2}}

(the isomorphisms are derived from Schensted’s bumping algorithm).
We know the general form for genuine highest weight elements for these crystals
[
KO21
, Proposition 7.1]
.

If we can prove

{\widetilde{W}_{\uparrow}}\otimes{\widetilde{V}_{\uparrow}}=R({V_{\uparrow}}\otimes{W_{\uparrow}})
and

{\widetilde{W}_{\downarrow}}\otimes{\widetilde{V}_{\downarrow}}=R({V_{\downarrow}}\otimes{W_{\downarrow}})

for the genuine highest weight states (using the notation from Theorem
4.14
) then
it will also hold for a general state,
since the crystal operators commute with the
R
-matrix.
Note that we can explicitly calculate
R({V_{\uparrow}}\otimes{W_{\uparrow}})
and
R({V_{\downarrow}}\otimes{W_{\downarrow}})

for the genuine highest weight states,
and we do so in Lemma
B.2
.
Then, by inspection, we see that

{\widetilde{W}_{\uparrow}}\otimes{\widetilde{V}_{\uparrow}}=R({V_{\uparrow}}\otimes{W_{\uparrow}})
and

{\widetilde{W}_{\downarrow}}\otimes{\widetilde{V}_{\downarrow}}=R({V_{\downarrow}}\otimes{W_{\downarrow}})

in Proposition
B.1
.

Lemma B.2
.

\displaystyle R\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}}(\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\,,

\displaystyle R\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}}(\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\,.

Proof.

\displaystyle\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L}\mathbf{2}^{L}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L}$}\\
\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|cc}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+L}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{3-3}\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol&\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{2}^{L}&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{3-3}\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol&\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{2}^{L}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}}(\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-L}\mathbf{2}^{L}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{3-3}\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol&\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{2}^{L}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}

The above two insertions are identical, proving the first part of the lemma.

\displaystyle\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\mathbf{0}^{M}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|c}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{0}^{M}&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|c}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{0}^{M}&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}}(\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-M}\mathbf{0}^{M}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|c}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{0}^{M}&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}

The above two insertions are identical, completing the proof.
∎

Note that applying
e_{i}
or
f_{i}

(with
i\in I\setminus\{\overline{0},\overline{m-r}\}
)
to a state will not change the positions of the solitons.
Note also that
H(e_{i}(x\otimes y))=H(x\otimes y)

and
H(f_{i}(x\otimes y))=H(x\otimes y)
by definition.
Therefore, if we prove that the phase shift of any genuine highest weight state
is
\delta=2\mathfrak{d}_{2}+H({V_{\downarrow}}\otimes{W_{\downarrow}})+H({V_{\uparrow}}\otimes{W_{\uparrow}})
,
then this formula must also hold in general.
We can compute the values of
H({V_{\downarrow}}\otimes{W_{\downarrow}})
and
H({V_{\uparrow}}\otimes{W_{\uparrow}})

for a genuine highest weight state explicitly, and do so in Lemma
B.3
.
It then suffices to show that the phase shift for a highest weight state is
2\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L-M
.

Lemma B.3
.

H\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)=-L,\qquad H\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)=-M.

Proof.

From the proof of Lemma
B.2
, we know

\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{3-3}\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol&\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{2}^{L}\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}.

There are
\mathfrak{d}_{2}(r-1)-L
boxes to the right of the

\max\{\mathfrak{d}_{1},\mathfrak{d}_{2}\}=\mathfrak{d}_{1}
column.
By definition, we have

H\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{3}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)=(\mathfrak{d}_{2}(r-1)-L)-\mathfrak{d}_{2}(r-1)=-L

as required.

From the proof of Lemma
B.2
, we know

\col\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}=\begin{array}[]{|c|c}\hline\cr\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\cline{2-2}\cr\mathbf{0}^{M}&\\
\cline{1-1}\cr\end{array}.

There are
\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M
boxes to the right of the
\max\{\mathfrak{d}_{1},\mathfrak{d}_{2}\}=\mathfrak{d}_{1}
column.
By definition, we have

H\left(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{1}^{\mathfrak{d}_{1}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-j})_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\right)=(\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M)-\mathfrak{d}_{2}\cdot 1=-M

as required.
∎

Finally, we have that
(T_{\ell})^{t}=(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{-t^{\prime}}(T_{\ell})^{t}(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t^{\prime}}

since time evolution operators commute by Proposition
3.5
,
(note that
(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{-1}
exists; see Remark
3.4
).
Therefore, if we prove the theorem for
T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1}
and choose
t^{\prime}
sufficiently large
(so that the solitons have finished colliding after applying
(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t^{\prime}}
)
and choose
t>t^{\prime}

(so that
(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{-t^{\prime}}(T_{\ell})^{t}
does not undo the collision;
this follows from Theorem
4.8
),
then we can prove the theorem in the general case.

To prove Proposition
B.1
, we could use direct (and tedious) computation.
However,
in the rest of this section we give an alternate proof,
in which we reduce the behaviour of the general system to the behaviour of a modified height
1
system.

B.2.
Behaviour of the
R
-matrix

It is first useful to classify how the
R
-matrix acts on common tableaux.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
\ell
be a positive integer.
Let
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}=\mathbf{3}^{a_{3}}\mathbf{2}^{a_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}}
and
(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}=\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}

for some positive integers
a_{3},a_{2},a_{1},b_{2},b_{1},b_{0}
such that
a_{3}+a_{2}+a_{1}=\ell
and
b_{2}+b_{1}+b_{0}=\ell
.

For

b=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\mathbf{y}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

with
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{0}
,
we have that
R(b)=

(
\mathbf{1}
a)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}^{a_{3}-1}\mathbf{2}^{a_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}+1}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{2}>0
;

(
\mathbf{1}
b)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{h}_{a_{3}+a_{2}}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{a_{3}+a_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}+1}\\
\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}-1}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{2}=0
and
b_{2}+b_{1}=a_{3}+a_{2}
and
b_{1}>0
.

For

b=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{2}\\
\mathbf{y}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

with
\mathbf{y}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
, we have that
R(b)=

(
\mathbf{2}
a)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{h}_{a_{3}+a_{2}}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{a_{3}+a_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}+1}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}+1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}-2}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{2}<a_{3}
and
b_{2}+b_{1}=a_{3}+a_{2}
and
b_{1}>1
and
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{0}
;

(
\mathbf{2}
b)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}^{a_{3}-1}\mathbf{2}^{a_{2}+1}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}-1}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{2}<a_{3}
and
b_{2}+b_{1}=a_{3}+a_{2}
and (
b_{1}=1
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
).

(
\mathbf{2}
c)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}^{a_{3}-1}\mathbf{2}^{a_{2}+2}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}-1}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
0<b_{2}=a_{3}
and
a_{1}>0
and (
b_{1}=0
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
);

(
\mathbf{2}
d)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}^{a_{3}-1}\mathbf{2}^{a_{2}+1}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
0<b_{2}=a_{3}
and
a_{1}=0
and (
b_{1}=0
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
).

For

b=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{y}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

with
\mathbf{y}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\mathbf{2}\}
we have that
R(b)=

(
\mathbf{3}
a)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{h}_{a_{3}+a_{2}}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{a_{3}+a_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}-1}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{1}>0
and
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{0}
;

(
\mathbf{3}
b)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}^{a_{3}}\mathbf{2}^{a_{2}+1}\mathbf{1}^{a_{1}-1}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{2}>0
and
a_{1}>0
and
\mathbf{y}\geq\mathbf{1}
and (
b_{1}=0
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
);

(
\mathbf{3}
c)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}^{a_{3}}\mathbf{2}^{a_{2}}\\
\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{2}>0
and
a_{1}=0
and
\mathbf{y}\geq\mathbf{1}
and (
b_{1}=0
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
);

(
\mathbf{3}
d)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{h}_{\ell}\\
\mathbf{z}_{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{3}(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell-1}\\
\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell-1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{2}
or (
b_{2}=0
and (
b_{1}=0
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
)).

The proof of the lemma follows by a direct application of Schensted’s bumping algorithm.
See Appendix
C
for details.

Remark B.5
.

The above lemma only lists the cases required to prove the main theorem
and does not completely classify the action of the
R
-matrix.

Remark B.6
.

In Lemma
B.4
,
\mathbf{X}
does not affect the action of
R
-matrix on genuine highest weight states.
Therefore, it is sufficient to only consider height
2
states.

Note that many cases of the lemma can be informally described using the height
1

R
-matrix:

Example B.7
.

Assuming
b_{1}>1
,

R(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array})=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}-1}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}
.
Compare this with the bottom row of
(

2

a)
:

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}+1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}-2}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}
.
Additionally, note that the second last row has an extra
\mathbf{1}
.
One interpretation of this is that when the carrier picks up the
\mathbf{2}

in the second last row, it gets swapped with a
\mathbf{1}
in the bottom row.

Example B.8
.

Assuming
b_{2}>0
,
\mathbf{y}\in\{\mathbf{1},\mathbf{0}\}
and (
b_{1}=0
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
),

R(\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{y}\\
\hline\cr\end{array})=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}
.
Compare this with the bottom row of
(

3

b)
:

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}^{b_{2}-1}\mathbf{1}^{b_{1}}\mathbf{y}\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}
.
Additionally, note that the second last row has an extra
\mathbf{2}
,
and has lost a
\mathbf{1}
.
One interpretation of this is that the carrier ‘should’ put down a
\mathbf{2}
,
but this
\mathbf{2}
is instead swapped with a
\mathbf{1}
from the second last row.

With these examples in mind, we can see
when the carrier picks up a
\mathbf{2}
in the second last row, it generally gets swapped with a
\mathbf{1}
in the bottom row;
and when the carrier ‘should’ put down a
\mathbf{2}
in the bottom row,
it generally gets swapped with a
\mathbf{1}
from the second last row.
Note that sometimes this happens simultaneously.
Note also that there are exceptions (
(

2

d)
for example).

This observation tells us that when computing time evolution
by moving the carrier through a genuine highest weight state,
the bottom row of the super BBS is similar to the single row case,
except for some swapping of
\mathbf{2}
and
\mathbf{1}
.
This swapping can occur simultaneously and is hard to keep track of.
Our strategy is to swap all of the
\mathbf{2}
from the second last row with
\mathbf{1}
from the bottom row

before
passing the carrier through,
so that no swapping occurs when passing the carrier through.
After employing this strategy to create a ‘swapped state’,
the bottom row would theoretically behave identically to the single row case.
This would reduce the problem of computing the time evolution to the single row case.
This is advantageous because the behaviour of the single row BBS is simpler and also easier to compute
since we can use an alternate algorithm for the
R
-matrix detailed in
[
HI00
, Section 2]
.
Unfortunately, the first soliton moves slower in the ‘swapped state’ than in the original state.
We can fix this by ‘speeding up’ the first soliton.

B.3.
L
-sped box-ball system

With this goal of ‘speeding up’ the first soliton, we define a new time evolution operator:

Definition B.9
.

Consider a general state

P=P_{1}\otimes P_{2}\otimes P_{3}\otimes\cdots=u_{1}^{\otimes c_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes c_{2}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{d_{2}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

where
\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr y_{d_{2}}&\cdots&y_{2}&y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}
is semistandard
and
\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{j}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\neq u_{1}
for any
j=1,\ldots,n
.
(Note that
d
or
c_{2}
can be
0
.)
For some non-negative integer
L
, set

P^{L}=u_{1}^{\otimes c_{1}}\otimes P_{c_{1}+d+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes P_{c_{1}+d+L}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\min(d,\ell-L)}\otimes P_{c_{1}+d+L+1}\otimes P_{c_{1}+d+L+2}\otimes\cdots.

Let
T_{\ell}(P^{L})=\widetilde{P}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{2}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{3}\otimes\cdots

and assume that
\widetilde{P}_{\widetilde{c}_{1}+1}
is the first non-vacuum element in
T_{\ell}(P^{L})
.
Define the
L
-sped time evolution operator
,
denoted
T_{\ell}^{L}
, by

T_{\ell}^{L}(P)=u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{c}_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d-\min(d,\ell-L)}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{\widetilde{c}_{1}+1}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{\widetilde{c}_{1}+2}\otimes\cdots.

Note that in the case where
d\leq\ell-L
, we have
T_{\ell}^{L}(P)=T_{\ell}(P^{L})
.

Example B.10
.

Let
L=2
and
\ell=5
, and
consider
T^{L}_{\ell}
acting on the state

P=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

We have that

P^{L}=u_{1}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Then,

T_{\ell}(P^{L})=u_{1}^{\otimes 5}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}
.
Therefore,

T^{L}_{\ell}(P)=u_{1}^{\otimes 5}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Note that we have only defined the
L
-sped time evolution operator for
some
two-soliton states.
This is sufficient for our purposes, but the definition can be extended using crystal operators if so desired.

Proposition B.11
.

Let

P=u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{2}+L}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

be an arbitrary height
1
genuine highest weight two soliton state
with
\mathfrak{c}_{2}+L\geq\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M
.
If
L\leq\mathfrak{d}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{1}>\mathfrak{d}_{2}
then,

(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1}^{L})^{t}(P)=u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{3}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{4}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-M}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

for
t
sufficiently large.
Moreover, the phase shift, as measured from the start of the solitons,
is
2\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M-L
.

Proof.

We follow a similar strategy as
[
HI00
]
and
[
FOY00
]
.
Consider applying the time evolution operator to
P
.
For
\mathfrak{c}_{2}
suficiently large,
the first soliton initially moves with speed
\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1
and the second soliton with speed
\mathfrak{d}_{2}
.
Eventually, we arrive at the state:

u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

By direct computation
(using either the
R
-matrix algorithm in Section
2.3
or in
[
HI00
, Section 2]
),
we find that after another
t^{\prime}
time units (with
t^{\prime}\leq\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}
),
this state becomes:

u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes t^{\prime}(\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1)}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-L-t^{\prime}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M+t^{\prime}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

When
t^{\prime}>\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}
the solitons do not interact,
and the first soliton moves with speed
\mathfrak{d}_{2}
and the second soliton moves with speed
\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1
.

Let
\delta_{3}
be the phase shift of the speed
\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1
soliton,
and
\delta_{2}
be the phase shift of the speed
\mathfrak{d}_{2}
soliton.
After time
t^{\prime}=\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}
, the phase shifts will remain the same,
so computing both phase shifts at time
t^{\prime}=\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}
, we find

\displaystyle\delta_{3}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}+(\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2})(\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1)+(\mathfrak{d}_{1}-L-\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2})+(\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M))-(\widetilde{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}+(\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2})(\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1))

\displaystyle=2\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M-L

\displaystyle\delta_{2}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}+(\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2})(\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1))-(\widetilde{\mathfrak{c}}_{1}+(\mathfrak{d}_{1}-L)+(\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M)+(\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2})\mathfrak{d}_{2})

\displaystyle=-(2\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M-L).

Thus, the total phase shift of the system is

\delta=\delta_{3}=-\delta_{2}=2\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M-L
.
∎

Note that Proposition
B.11
essentially shows us that two-soliton states behave solitonically
with respect to
L
-sped time evolution.
Our aim is to reduce the behaviour of the general super BBS to the behaviour of a height
1

L
-sped BBS.

B.4.
Reducing to height 1

As alluded to in Section
B.2
,
we can define a map from a genuine highest weight super BBS state to a
L
-sped state
by swapping all of the
\mathbf{1}
in the bottom row with
\mathbf{2}
in the second last row,
and then removing all but the bottom row.
More precisely,
assuming
d_{1}-L>0
,
the super BBS state

u_{1}^{\otimes C_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-Q}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes C_{2}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
y_{Q}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
y_{Q+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
y_{L}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
y_{L+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
y_{d_{2}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

(recall that we are omitting
\mathbf{X}
as per Remark
B.6
),
where
y_{1}y_{2}\cdots y_{d_{2}}=\mathbf{0}^{M}\mathbf{1}^{d_{2}-M}
and (
Q>0
only if
C_{2}=0
),
is mapped to the
L
-sped state

u_{1}^{\otimes C_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes C_{2}+L-Q}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{d_{2}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Call this map
\mathcal{F}
.

Example B.12
.

Let

P=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Then,
\mathcal{F}(P)=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}
.

The map
\mathcal{F}
is invertible.
Indeed,
\mathcal{F}^{-1}
maps the
L
-sped state

u_{1}^{\otimes c_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes c_{2}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr y_{d_{2}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty},

where
d>0
,
to the super BBS state

u_{1}^{\otimes c_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-Q}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\max(0,c_{2}-L)}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
y_{Q}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
y_{Q+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
y_{L}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
y_{L+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
y_{d_{2}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty},

where
d_{1}=d+L
and
Q=\max(0,L-c_{2})
.

Remark B.13
.

In defining the domain of
\mathcal{F}
,
we assumed that
d_{1}-L>0
.
This assumption ensures that
\mathcal{F}^{-1}
is well-defined.
We sometimes encounter states where
d_{1}=L

and we need to deal with these edge cases separately.

Recalling that

u_{1}=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array},

we can alternatively compute
\mathcal{F}^{-1}

by the following process:

(1)

Add a row above the
L
-sped state, and fill it entirely with
\mathbf{3}

(if needed, add rows filled with
\mathbf{X}
above this added row).

(2)

In the second soliton, replace the first
L
positions of this added row with
\mathbf{2}
.

(3)

Replace the first
L
occurrences of
\mathbf{2}
that occur immediately after the first soliton
(regardless of the row they appear in) with
\mathbf{1}
.

Proposition B.14
.

Let
P
be the state

u_{1}^{\otimes C_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-Q}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes C_{2}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
y_{Q}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
y_{Q+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
y_{L}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
y_{L+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
y_{d_{2}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

where
y_{1}y_{2}\cdots y_{d_{2}}=\mathbf{0}^{M}\mathbf{1}^{d_{2}-M}
and

C_{2}+L-Q\geq\min(d_{1},\ell)-1-M

and (
Q>0
only if
C_{2}=0
).
Further assume that
d_{1}-L>0
and that
if
C_{2}+L-Q=\min(d_{1},\ell)-1-M
then
d_{1}-L-1>0
.
Then, for
\ell\leq d_{2}+1
and
L,M<\ell
,
we have
T_{\ell}(P)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}T^{L}_{\ell}\mathcal{F}(P)
.

The only states encountered when evolving a genuine highest weight super BBS are the form
P

(or of the form discussed in Remark
B.13
);
this will be a consequence of the proof of Proposition
B.14
.
Therefore, Proposition
B.14
allows us to compute the time evolution
of a genuine highest weight super BBS state using almost entirely height
1

L
-sped states.
Since
L
-sped states behave solitonically by Proposition
B.11
,
this will complete the proof of Proposition
B.1
and hence
Theorem
4.14
(except in the edge cases of Remark
B.13
).

To prove Proposition
B.14
, first observe that

\mathcal{F}(P)=u_{1}^{\otimes C_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes C_{2}+L-Q}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{2}-M}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Since
R(u_{\ell}\otimes u_{1})=u_{1}\otimes u_{\ell}
,
(for
u_{1},\,u_{\ell}
of any height)
and
\mathcal{F}^{-1}
does not change
u_{1}^{\otimes C_{1}}
,
the result holds for the first
C_{1}
tableaux of the states.
We can thus assume, without loss of generality, that
C_{1}=0
.
Additionally,
if
C_{2}\geq\min(d_{1},\ell)
then the solitons do not interact in either system at this time step,
and the result is obvious.
So, assume
C_{2}<\min(d_{1},\ell)
.

Let
\widetilde{C}=\min(d_{1},\ell)
.
If
C_{2}+L-Q>\widetilde{C}-1-M
we find that

(B.2)

T^{L}_{\ell}\mathcal{F}(P)=u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{C}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes C_{2}+L-Q+(\min(d_{2},\ell)-\widetilde{C})}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{2}-M}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

and if
C_{2}+L-Q=\widetilde{C}-1-M
we find that

(B.3)

T^{L}_{\ell}\mathcal{F}(P)=u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{C}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-L-1}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\ell-1-M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{2}-M+1}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

(noting that
\min(\ell-1-M,d_{2}-M)=\ell-1-M
).

Let
P=P_{1}\otimes P_{2}\otimes P_{3}\otimes\cdots

and let
u(j)
denote the carrier after applying the
R
-matrix to
P

j
-times.
That is,

R_{j-1}\cdots R_{1}R_{0}(u_{\ell}\otimes P)=\widetilde{P}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\widetilde{P}_{j}\otimes u(j)\otimes P_{j+1}\otimes P_{j+2}\otimes\cdots

for some
\widetilde{P}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{P}_{j}
.
Note that
u(0)=u_{\ell}
.
Let

u(j)=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}^{A_{3}(j)}\mathbf{2}^{A_{2}(j)}\mathbf{1}^{A_{1}(j)}\\
\mathbf{2}^{B_{2}(j)}\mathbf{1}^{B_{1}(j)}\mathbf{0}^{B_{0}(j)}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

where
A_{3},A_{2},A_{1},B_{2},B_{1},B_{0}
are functions on non-negative integers.
We will often write
A_{3}
for
A_{3}(j)
, with
j
being clear from context.
Similarly for the other functions.

From Lemma
B.4

(

3

c)
,
with the assumption
C_{1}=0
,
we find that

\widetilde{P}_{1}=\cdots=\widetilde{P}_{\min(d_{1}-Q,\ell)}=u_{1}

and

u(\min(d_{1}-Q,\ell))=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}^{\ell}\\
\mathbf{2}^{B_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{B_{1}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

with
B_{1}=\min(d_{1}-Q,\ell)
and
B_{2}=\ell-B_{1}
.
If
Q=0
, then we clearly have
\widetilde{P}_{1}=\cdots=\widetilde{P}_{\min(d_{1},\ell)}=u_{1}
,
and if
Q>0
(so that
C_{2}=0
) then, by
(

1

a)
, these equalities also hold.
That is, the first
\widetilde{C}=\min(d_{1},\ell)
tableaux in
T_{\ell}(P)
are
u_{1}
.
This matches the first
\widetilde{C}
tableaux in
\mathcal{F}^{-1}T^{L}_{\ell}\mathcal{F}(P)

(cf. (
B.2
) and (
B.3
)).
Note that

u(\widetilde{C})=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}^{\ell-A_{1}}\mathbf{1}^{A_{1}}\\
\mathbf{2}^{B_{2}}\mathbf{1}^{B_{1}}\mathbf{0}^{A_{1}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

where
B_{1}=\min(d_{1}-Q,\ell)
,
A_{1}=\widetilde{C}-B_{1}
and
B_{2}=\ell-\widetilde{C}
.

In the case where
d_{1}\leq\ell
and
C_{2}>0
or in the case where
d_{1}-Q>\ell
, we have

(B.4)

\widetilde{P}_{\widetilde{C}+1}=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

by
(

3

d)
.
If
d_{1}-Q\leq\ell<d_{1}
(which implies
Q>0
),
then (
B.4
) holds by
(

1

b)
.
If
d_{1}\leq\ell
and
C_{2}=0
, then by
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

3

a)
or
(

3

b)
,
we have that (
B.4
) holds.
Note that
(

2

c)
and
(

2

d)
do not apply, since
B_{1}=d_{1}-Q\geq d_{1}-L>0

and
B_{2}+B_{1}=A_{3}
, so we cannot have
B_{2}=A_{3}
.

(

3

c)
and
(

3

d)
do not apply,
for otherwise
C_{2}=0

and
M=Q
(since
B_{1}=d_{1}-Q>0
which would imply
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
),
so
C_{2}+L-Q\geq\widetilde{C}-1-M
if and only if
L\geq\widetilde{C}-1=d_{1}-1
.
But since
d_{1}-L>0
, we must have
L=d_{1}-1
.
Then we have
C_{2}+L-Q=\widetilde{C}-1-M
,
which implies that
d_{1}-L-1>0
by assumption, a contradiction.

Therefore,
the
(\widetilde{C}+1)
-th tableaux in
T_{\ell}(P)

is the same as the
(\widetilde{C}+1)
-th tableaux in
\mathcal{F}^{-1}T^{M}_{\ell}\mathcal{F}(P)
.
In particular, the first soliton starts at the same point in both of these states.

By
(

3

d)
and
(

1

b)
, we have that

\widetilde{P}_{j}=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\qquad\text{and}\qquad u(d_{1}+C_{2})=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}^{\ell-{A_{1}}}\mathbf{1}^{A_{1}}\\
\mathbf{2}^{\ell-B_{1}-A_{1}}\mathbf{1}^{B_{1}}\mathbf{0}^{A_{1}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

for
\widetilde{C}+1\leq j\leq d_{1}+C_{2}
,
A_{1}=Q
and
B_{1}=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q
.
Observe that
B_{1}>0
.
Indeed, if
A_{1}=Q>0
then
C_{2}=0
,
and the fact that
Q<\widetilde{C}
(which follows from
Q\leq L<\ell
and
Q\leq L<d_{1}
)
implies that
B_{1}=\widetilde{C}-Q>0
.
Alternatively, if
A_{1}=Q=0
then the assumption that
C_{2}<\min(d_{1},\ell)=\widetilde{C}
implies that

B_{1}=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}>0
.
Note that a consequence of
B_{1}>0
is that
B_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2})<A_{3}(d_{1}+C_{2})
.

Now, we investigate what happens when we move the carrier starting from
u(d_{1}+C_{2})

and pausing when we reach
u((d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+L)
.
We find that Case
(

2

a)
applies until
B_{1}=1
or we reach
u((d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+M)

(note that since
B_{2}+B_{1}=A_{3}
and
B_{1}>0
when applying Case
(

2

a)
, we always have
B_{2}<A_{3}
);
then Case
(

2

b)
applies until
B_{2}=A_{3}

(note that, in Case
(

2

b)
, if
B_{1}(j)=1
then
A_{3}(j)=B_{2}(j)+1
,
so if
B_{1}(j+1)=0
then
A_{3}(j+1)=B_{2}(j)=B_{2}(j+1)
);
Case
(

2

c)
applies until
A_{1}=0
; and from then onwards,
(

2

d)
applies.

Remark B.15
.

Note that the only way to leave
(

2

d)
is to have
B_{2}=A_{3}=0
.
Since
A_{3}
is always decremented by one in
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

2

c)
, and
(

2

d)
,
to have
A_{3}=0
we would need
(d_{1}-Q+C_{2}+L)-(d_{1}+C_{2})=L-Q\geq A_{3}(d_{1}+C_{2})=\ell-Q
.
However, this inequality does not hold, because we assumed
L<\ell
.
We have thus shown that
(

2

d)
is the last possible case,
and consequently that these four cases are the only four which we can encounter.
However, note that some of these cases may be skipped,
and that we might not reach the last case before we reach
u((d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+L)
.

Next, we investigate what happens when we move the carrier starting from
u((d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+L)

and continuing until we complete the time evolution.
We find that Case
(

3

a)
applies until
B_{1}=0
or we reach
u((d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+M)
;
then Case
(

3

b)
applies until
B_{2}=0
or
A_{1}=0
, or until we reach
u((d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+d_{2})
;
Case
(

3

c)
applies until
B_{2}=0
or until we reach
u((d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+d_{2})
;
and from then onwards,
(

3

d)
applies.
Observe that
(

3

a)
can only be preceded by
(

2

a)
or
(

3

a)
;
and that
(

3

b)
can only be preceded by
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

2

c)
,
(

3

a)
or
(

3

b)
.

Remark B.16
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
B.4
,
Cases
(

3

a)
,
(

3

b)
,
(

3

c)
and
(

3

d)

are the only possible cases.
However, note that some of these cases may be skipped.

Observation B.17
.

The cases which put down a
\mathbf{1}
in the bottom row occur before
the cases which put down a
\mathbf{2}
in the bottom row.
Moreover, the cases following a
\mathbf{2}
in the bottom row
only put down a
\mathbf{2}
(except in the final case,
(

3

d)
).
Only
\mathbf{3}
’s are put down in the second last row,
except in
(

3

d)
.

Let
N(j)
be the number of
\mathbf{1}
’s in
\widetilde{P}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\widetilde{P}_{j}
.

Lemma B.18
.

For every case except
(

3

c)
and
(

3

d)
, we have
d_{1}=N+\max(B_{1}-A_{2},0)+A_{1}
.

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle N(d_{1}+C_{2})+B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})-A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2})+A_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})

\displaystyle=(d_{1}+C_{2}-\widetilde{C})+(\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q)-0+Q

\displaystyle=d_{1}.

We proceed by induction.

•

If Case
(

2

a)
applies to
u(j)
,
then
N(j+1)=N(j)+1
,
B_{1}(j+1)=B_{1}(j)-2
,
A_{2}(j+1)=0
,
A_{1}(j+1)=A_{1}(j)+1
,
so
N(j+1)+B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)+A_{1}(j+1)=d_{1}
by the inductive hypothesis
(noting that
B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)\geq 0
).

Observe that
B_{2}(j+1)+B_{1}(j+1)=A_{3}(j+1)+A_{2}(j+1)
,
so if
B_{2}(j+1)=A_{3}(j+1)
then
B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)=0
.

•

If Case
(

2

b)
applies to
u(j)
and
j\geq(d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+M
(i.e.
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
),
then
N(j+1)=N(j)+1
,
B_{1}(j+1)=B_{1}(j)
,
A_{2}(j+1)=A_{2}(j)+1
,
A_{1}(j+1)=A_{1}(j)
,
so
N(j+1)+B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)+A_{1}(j+1)=d_{1}
by the inductive hypothesis.
Since
(

2

b)
decreases
B_{1}-A_{2}
by
1
,
and
B_{1}(j)-A_{2}(j)=A_{3}(j)-B_{2}(j)>0
,
we have that
B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)\geq 0
.

Alternatively, if
j<(d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+M
(i.e.
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{1}
) then
B_{1}(j)=1
.
Since
1-A_{2}(j)=B_{1}(j)-A_{2}(j)=A_{3}(j)-B_{2}(j)>0
,
we have that
A_{2}(j)=0
.
Thus,
B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)=-1
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle N(j+1)+\max(B_{1}(j+1)+A_{2}(j+1),0)+A_{1}(j+1)

\displaystyle=(N(j)+1)+0+A_{1}(j)

\displaystyle=N(j)+B_{1}(j)-A_{2}(j)+A_{1}(j)

\displaystyle=d_{1}.

Observe that, if
j\geq(d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+M

then
B_{2}(j+1)+B_{1}(j+1)=A_{3}(j+1)+A_{2}(j+1)
.
And, if
j<(d_{1}-Q)+C_{2}+M
then
B_{1}(j+1)=0
.
In either case, if
A_{3}(j+1)=B_{2}(j+1)
then
B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)\leq 0
.

•

Assume Case
(

2

a)
or
(

2

b)

applies to
u(j_{0})
and Case
(

2

c)
applies to
u(j_{0}+1)
.
(Note that the first
(

2

c)

must
be preceded by
(

2

a)
or
(

2

b)
because
B_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2})<A_{3}(d_{1}+C_{2})
.)
Since we have
A_{3}(j_{0}+1)=B_{2}(j_{0}+1)
,
we have
B_{1}(j_{0}+1)-A_{2}(j_{0}+1)\leq 0
,
as noted in the above cases.

That is, the first application of
(

2

c)
has
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0
.
Since further applications of
(

2

c)
only decrease
the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
, we have

N+\max(B_{1}-A_{2},0)+A_{1}=N+A_{1}
.
Since
(

2

c)
increases
N
by
1
and decreases
A_{1}
by
1
,
we have
N+A_{1}=d_{1}
by the inductive hypothesis.

•

Assume
u(j_{0}+1)
is the first carrier to which Case
(

2

d)
applies.
If Case
(

2

a)
or
(

2

b)
applied to
u(j_{0})
,
then we have
B_{1}(j_{0}+1)-A_{2}(j_{0}+1)\leq 0
since
A_{3}(j_{0}+1)=B_{2}(j_{0}+1)
, as noted in the above cases.
If Case
(

2

c)
applies to
u(j_{0})
then,
as above, we have that
B_{1}(j_{0}+1)-A_{2}(j_{0}+1)\leq 0
.
(Note that the first
(

2

d)

must
be preceded by
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
or
(

2

c)
because
B_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2})<A_{3}(d_{1}+C_{2})
.)

Since
(

2

d)
can only decrease the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
,
we have that
B_{1}(j)-A_{2}(j)\leq 0
for
j>j_{0}
.
Therefore

N(j+1)+\max(B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1),0)+A_{1}(j+1)=N(j)+A_{1}(j)=d_{1}

by the inductive hypothesis.

•

Case
(

3

a)
can only be preceded by Case
(

2

a)
or else is the first case.
Regardless,
A_{2}=0
for
(

3

a)
.
Therefore,
B_{1}-A_{2}\geq 0
.
Moreover, if
(

3

a)
applies to
u(j)

then
N(j+1)=N(j)+1,\,B_{1}(j+1)=B_{1}(j)-1,\,A_{2}(j+1)=0,\,A_{1}(j+1)=A_{1}(j)
.
Therefore,
N(j+1)+B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)+A_{1}(j+1)=d_{1}
by the inductive hypothesis.

•

Assume
(

3

b)
applies to
u(j)
.
If
B_{1}(j)=0
then
B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1)\leq B_{1}(j)-A_{2}(j)\leq 0
,
so
\max(B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1),0)=0
.
If
B_{1}(j)\neq 0
, we have that
B_{1}(j+1)=B_{1}(j)+1
and
A_{2}(j+1)=A_{2}(j)+1
,
so
\max(B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1),0)=\max(B_{1}(j)-A_{2}(j),0)
.

Additionally,
N(j+1)=N(j)+1
and
A_{1}(j+1)=A_{1}(j)-1
.
Therefore,

N(j+1)+\max(B_{1}(j+1)-A_{2}(j+1),0)+A_{1}(j+1)=d_{1}

by the inductive hypothesis. ∎

Lemma B.19
.

If
C_{2}+L-Q>\widetilde{C}-1-M
then
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0
immediately before the first
(

3

c)
/
(

3

d)
.
If
C_{2}+L-Q=\widetilde{C}-1-M
then
B_{1}-A_{2}=1
immediately before the first
(

3

c)
/
(

3

d)
.

Proof.

We consider three cases.

•

Assume
C_{2}+L-Q>\widetilde{C}-M-1
.
Recall that
B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q
and
A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2})=0
.
We can apply
(

2

a)

\lfloor B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})/2\rfloor
times or
M-Q

or
L-Q
times, whichever is smaller.

If we apply
(

2

a)

\lfloor B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})/2\rfloor
times,
then
B_{1}=0
or
B_{1}=1
(and
A_{2}=0
).
If
B_{1}=0
then we are done,
since
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0
and the cases afterwards can only decrease the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
.
If
B_{1}=1
and Case
(

2

b)
or
(

3

a)
applied,
then the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
will decrease by
1
or more,
so
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0
.
If Case
(

2

c)
or
(

2

d)
applied,
then we would already have
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0

(as in the proof of Lemma
B.18
).
Also note that if
B_{1}=1
then
(

3

b)
,
(

3

c)
,
(

3

d)
do not apply,
for otherwise we would have

\lfloor B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})/2\rfloor=(\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-1)/2=L-Q=M-Q

and hence
\widetilde{C}-M-1=C_{2}+L-Q
,
contradicting
\widetilde{C}-M-1<C_{2}+L-Q
.
After the
B_{1}=1
case, the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
can only decrease.

Alternatively, if we apply
(

2

a)

M-Q
times, then

B_{1}=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-2(M-Q)\leq L-M
(and
A_{2}=0
).
If we ever apply
(

2

c)
or
(

2

d)
then
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0
,
as in the proof of Lemma
B.18
.
If we never apply
(

2

c)
or
(

2

d)
,
then we must apply
(

2

b)

L-M
times.
Thus,
B_{1}-A_{2}=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-2(M-Q)-(L-M)\leq 0
.
The remaining cases can only decrease the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
.

If we apply
(

2

a)

L-Q
times, then

B_{1}=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-2(L-Q)\leq M-L
(and
A_{2}=0
).
Then, we apply
(

3

a)

\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-2(L-Q)
times,
so we have
B_{1}=0
and
A_{2}=0
.
Since
(

3

b)
can only decrease the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
,
we have that
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0
.

•

Assume
C_{2}+L-Q=\widetilde{C}-1-M
and
L\geq M
.
Recall that
B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q
and
A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2})=0
.
We can apply
(

2

a)

\lfloor B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})/2\rfloor
times or
M-Q
times,
whichever is smaller.
Observe that
\widetilde{C}+Q-C_{2}=L+M+1\geq 2M+1
, so
\widetilde{C}-Q-C_{2}\geq 2(M-Q)+1
.
Therefore,
M-Q\leq\lfloor B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})/2\rfloor
and we can only apply
(

2

a)

M-Q
times.
We find
B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2}+M-Q)=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-2(M-Q)
and
A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2}+M-Q)=0
.
Then, we apply
(

2

b)

L-M
times,
with
B_{1}
remaining the same and
A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2}+L-Q)=L-M
.
That is,

B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2}+L-Q)-A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2}+L-Q)=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}+Q-M-L=1.

(Note that we were able to apply
(

2

b)

L-M
times because
B_{1}-A_{2}>0
;
we would have
B_{1}-A_{2}\leq 0
when applying
(

2

c)
or
(

2

d)
,
as in the proof of Lemma
B.18
.)
Then we apply
(

3

b)
, which does not change the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
in this case.

•

Assume
C_{2}+L-Q=\widetilde{C}-1-M
and
L<M
.
Recall that
B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q
and
A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2})=0
.
We can apply
(

2

a)

\lfloor B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})/2\rfloor
times or
L-Q
times,
whichever is smaller.
Observe that
\widetilde{C}-C_{2}+Q=L+M+1>2L+1
, so
\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q>2(L-Q)+1
.
Therefore,
L-Q\leq\lfloor B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2})/2\rfloor
and we can only apply
(

2

a)

L-Q
times.
We find
B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2}+L-Q)=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-2(L-Q)
and
A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2}+L-Q)=0
.
Then, we apply
(

3

a)

M-L
times,
to get
B_{1}(d_{1}+C_{2}+M-Q)=\widetilde{C}-C_{2}-Q-2(L-Q)-(M-L)=1
and
A_{2}(d_{1}+C_{2}+M-Q)=0
.
Next, we apply
(

3

b)
, which does not change the value of
B_{1}-A_{2}
in this case.
∎

From Observation
B.17
,
we can conclude that the tableaux in
T_{\ell}(P)
before we reach Case
(

3

d)

are of the form

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\widetilde{C}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\widetilde{d}_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\widetilde{C}_{2}}.

for some non-negative integers
\widetilde{d}_{1}
and
\widetilde{C}_{2}
.
Observe that
(

3

d)
puts down the columns of the carrier in reverse order,
so we simply need to examine the carrier
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})
.

Observation B.20
.

The value of
A_{2}+A_{1}
is incremented by
1

in cases
(

1

a)
,
(

1

b)
,
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

2

c)
,
(

2

d)

and the value of
A_{2}+A_{1}
stays the same in cases
(

3

a)
,
(

3

b)
,
(

3

c)
.

Observation
B.20
tells us that

A_{2}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})+A_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})=L
.
Therefore, the number of
\mathbf{1}
and
\mathbf{2}
in the second last row of
T_{\ell}(P)
is
L
,
so
\mathcal{F}T_{\ell}(P)
is a
L
-sped state.

Now, we determine the value of
\widetilde{d}_{1}
by examining
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1})
.
Observe that
(

3

c)
and
(

3

d)
do not apply before
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1})
,
so Lemma
B.18
applies.
That is, at
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1})
we have
d_{1}=N+\max(B_{1}-A_{2},0)+A_{1}
.
Rearranging, we get
\widetilde{d}_{1}=N=d_{1}-A_{1}-\max(B_{1}-A_{2},0)
.
By Lemma
B.19
,

\max(B_{1}-A_{2},0)=\begin{cases}0&\text{if}~C_{2}+L-Q>\widetilde{C}-1-M\\
1&\text{if}~C_{2}+L-Q=\widetilde{C}-1-M\end{cases}.

If
\widetilde{C}_{2}>0
, then
(

2

d)
or
(

3

c)
applies to
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1})
;
in both of these cases we must have
A_{1}=0
, which implies
\widetilde{d}_{1}=d_{1}-\max(B_{1}-A_{2},0)
.
We find that the first
\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}
factors in
\mathcal{F}T_{\ell}(P)
are

(B.5)

\begin{cases}u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{C}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes L}&\text{if}~C_{2}+L-Q>\widetilde{C}-1-M\\
u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{C}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes d_{1}-L-1}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes L}&\text{if}~C_{2}+L-Q=\widetilde{C}-1-M\end{cases}

which matches (
B.2
) and (
B.3
).
If
\widetilde{C}_{2}=0
then
(

3

d)
applies to
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1})
,
and the columns are put down in reverse order.
Thus, in
T_{\ell}(P)
, there are
A_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1})
factors with
\mathbf{1}
in the second last row,
and
\widetilde{d}_{1}
factors
\mathbf{1}
in the bottom row immediately before them.
Therefore, the first
\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}
factors in
\mathcal{F}T_{\ell}(P)

are
u_{1}^{\otimes\widetilde{C}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\widetilde{d}_{1}+A_{1}-L}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes L-A_{1}}

(note that
A_{1}\leq L
by Observation
B.20
).
Since
\widetilde{d}_{1}+A_{1}=d_{1}-\max(B_{1}-A_{2},0)
,
this is the same as (
B.2
) and (
B.3
).

Now, consider
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})
.
Let
D=\max(B_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1})-A_{2}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}),0)
.
Observe that we have
d_{1}=\widetilde{d}_{1}+D+A_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})
.
But, we have a total of

\widetilde{d}_{1}+B_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})+A_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})

appearances of
\mathbf{1}

in the state or carrier at
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})
.
Therefore,

(B.6)

(\widetilde{d}_{1}+B_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})+A_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2}))-(\widetilde{d}_{1}+D+A_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2}))=B_{1}(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})-D

of the
\mathbf{1}
have come from the second soliton’s bottom row in
P
.

Since
(

3

d)
applies to
u(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2})
,
we must have
\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2}=d_{1}+C_{2}+d_{2}-Q
or
B_{2}=0
(with
B_{1}=0
or
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
).
We claim that
\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2}=d_{1}+C_{2}+\min(\ell-D,d_{2})-Q
.
Indeed:

•

In the case
\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2}=d_{1}+C_{2}+d_{2}-Q
,
all of the
\mathbf{1}
from the second soliton have been picked up by the carrier,
so
B_{1}-D=d_{2}-M
(using (
B.6
)).
The carrier also has
M
copies of
\mathbf{0}
,
so we have that
\ell\geq B_{1}+M=(d_{2}-M+D)+M
;
in particular,
d_{2}\leq\ell-D
.

•

In the case
B_{2}=0
we have
B_{1}+B_{0}=\ell
.
If
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{2}
then the previous case applies,
so assume
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{2}
.
If
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
, then the carrier has picked up all of the

\mathbf{0}
, so
B_{0}=M
.
If
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{0}
, then we must have
B_{1}=0
for
(

3

d)
to apply, and so
\ell=B_{0}\leq M
,
contradicting
M<\ell
.
We deduce that, in this case,
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{1}
and
B_{0}=M
.
Since
B_{1}-D=(\ell-M)-D
of the
\mathbf{1}
’s have come from the second soliton
(using (
B.6
)), we can deduce that

\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2}=d_{1}+C_{2}+(M-Q)+(\ell-M-D)=d_{1}+C_{2}+\ell-D-Q
.
Moreover, since
\ell-M-D
of the
\mathbf{1}
come from the
d_{2}-M
copies of
\mathbf{1}
in
the second soliton, we have
\ell-M-D\leq d_{2}-M
which implies
\ell-D\leq d_{2}
.

We conclude that
\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{d}_{1}+\widetilde{C}_{2}=d_{1}+C_{2}+\min(\ell-D,d_{2})-Q
.
Comparing with (
B.2
) and (
B.3
),
this is what we desire.
That is, the second soliton in
\mathcal{F}T_{\ell}(P)
starts at the same place
as the second soliton in
T^{L}_{\ell}\mathcal{F}(P)
.
Since the first solitons in both of these are the same length,
we have that the second solitons are also the same length.
Since
(

3

d)
puts the columns down in reverse order,
we can thus deduce that

\mathcal{F}T_{\ell}(P)=T^{L}_{\ell}\mathcal{F}(P).

This proves Proposition
B.14
.

Observe that
T_{\ell}(P)
satisfies the assumptions of Proposition
B.14
,
except we may have
d_{1}-L=0
or
d_{1}-L-1=0
with
C_{2}+L-Q=\min(d_{1},\ell)-1-M
.

B.5.
Edge cases

As previously noted,
Propositions
B.14
and
B.11

together prove the main theorem except in edge cases.
It is not difficult to see that
these edge cases are exactly the genuine highest weight states in which
L=\mathfrak{d}_{2}
.

Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a genuine highest weight state (as in (
B.1
)) in which
L=\mathfrak{d}_{2}
,
and let
t_{0}=((\mathfrak{c}_{2}+L)-(\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M))+(\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2})=\mathfrak{c}_{2}+M+\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}
.
We can use repeated applications of Proposition
B.14
to compute

(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t_{0}-1}(\mathfrak{p})=u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{1}+(\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1)(t_{0}-1)}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M+1}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}-1}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

At this point, we can no longer apply Proposition
B.14
.
So, we manually compute the time steps after this point using Lemma
B.4
.

If
t_{0}\leq t\leq t_{0}+M
then
(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t}(\mathfrak{p})
is:

u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{1}+(\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1)t_{0}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}(t-t_{0})}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M+(t-t_{0})}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{1}\\
\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M-(t-t_{0})}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes t-t_{0}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Indeed, if
t=t_{0}
, we simply apply
(

3

d)
,
(

3

c)
,
(

1

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

3

d)

to
(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t_{0}-1}(\mathfrak{p})
.
If
t_{0}<t\leq t_{0}+M
then, to
(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t-1}
, we first apply
(

3

d)
,
(

3

c)
,
(

1

a)
.
Then, we apply
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

2

c)
— note that we apply
(

2

a)
and
(

2

c)
the same number of times. Finally, we apply
(

3

b)
once and then
(

3

d)
for the remainder.

If
t>t_{0}+M
then
(T_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1})^{t}(\mathfrak{p})
is:

u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{1}+(\mathfrak{d}_{2}+1)t_{0}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}(t-t_{0})}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes t-(t_{0}+M)}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\mathbf{0}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{2}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{3}\\
\mathbf{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}-\mathfrak{d}_{2}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}.

Indeed, this is obvious for
t>t_{0}+M+\mathfrak{d}_{2}
.
If
t\leq t_{0}+M+\mathfrak{d}_{2}
we apply
(

3

d)
,
(

3

c)
,
(

3

d)
.
Then we apply
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

2

c)
,
(

2

d)
— note that, during this computation,
a_{3}-b_{2}+a_{1}
is the number of
\mathbf{1}
in the carrier from the first soliton;
that
a_{3}-b_{2}+a_{1}<\mathfrak{d}_{2}
at the start of this computation;
and that
a_{3}-b_{2}+a_{1}
is decreased by one in
(

2

a)
,
(

2

b)
,
(

2

c)
.
Finally,
(

3

c)
applies once and then
(

3

d)
applies for the remainder.

Therefore, the case where
L=\mathfrak{d}_{2}
behaves solitonically.
Moreover, we can easily calculate
that the phase shift is
\delta=\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M=2\mathfrak{d}_{2}-L-M
.
This takes care of the edge cases and completes the proof of Theorem
4.14
.

Remark B.21
.

We could possibly reformulate
L
-sped solitons to
allow size
0
(i.e. speed
L
) solitons.
This would have the benefit of removing the edge cases,
but would also make the definitions and proofs more technical.

B.6.
The
m=r+1
case

In Section
B.1
we assumed that
m\geq r+2
.
All that remains is to prove the
m=r+1
and
m=r
cases.
(Recall that we will not consider
m<r
, see Remark
3.3
.)

For the
m=r+1
case,
we can define
\mathbf{X},\,\mathbf{3},\,\mathbf{2},\mathbf{1}
as in Subsection
B.1

(note that
\mathbf{3}=\overline{3}
,
\mathbf{2}=\overline{2}
and
\mathbf{1}=\overline{1}
).
The genuine highest weight states are of the form:

\mathfrak{p}=u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{3}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}^{\otimes\mathfrak{d}_{1}}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{2}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{w}_{1}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{y}_{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathbf{X}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{2}}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{j}=\begin{cases}\overline{2}&\text{if }j\leq L\\
\overline{3}&\text{if }j>L\end{cases},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{y}_{j}=\begin{cases}M+1-j&\text{if }j\leq M\\
\overline{1}&\text{if }j>M\end{cases},

for some non-negative integers
L,M\leq\mathfrak{d}_{2}
.
Note that the value of
n
may limit which of these highest weight states are possible.
Note also that there are similarities with the genuine highest weight states for the
m\geq r+2
case.
Lemma
B.4
can be adapted for
m=r+1
by:

•

replacing
\mathbf{0}^{b_{0}}
with
(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=b_{2}+b_{1}+1}^{\ell}

where
\mathbf{z}_{b_{2}+b_{1}+1}\geq 1

and
\mathbf{z}_{j}=\mathbf{z}_{b_{2}+b_{1}+1}+j-(b_{2}+b_{1}+1)
for
j\geq p+q+1
;

•

replacing any instance of ‘
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{0}
’ with ‘
1\leq\mathbf{y}<\mathbf{z}_{b_{2}+b_{1}+1}
’; and

•

replacing ‘
\mathbf{y}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
’ with ‘
\mathbf{1}\leq\mathbf{y}<\mathbf{z}_{b_{2}+b_{1}+1}
’
and replacing ‘
\mathbf{y}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\mathbf{2}\}
’ with ‘
\mathbf{2}\leq\mathbf{y}<\mathbf{z}_{b_{1}+b_{2}+1}
’.

After these modifications, the proof of Lemma
B.4

remains similar to Appendix
C
.
Then, with some care, the arguments for the
m\geq r+2
case can be adapted to prove Theorem
4.14
in the
m=r+1
case.

B.7.
The
m=r
case

The
m=r
case is slightly different than the other cases,
owing to the fact that the genuine highest weight states have a different form:

\mathfrak{p}=u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\overline{2}\\
\mathfrak{y}_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\overline{2}\\
\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-M}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\overline{2}\\
\mathfrak{x}_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\overline{2}\\
\mathfrak{x}_{M}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\mathfrak{c}_{2}}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-M+1}\\
\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{1}-M+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathfrak{w}_{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\\
\mathfrak{y}_{\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{y}_{j}=\mathfrak{d}_{1}+\mathfrak{d}_{2}-M+1-j.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{x}_{j}=M+1-j,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{j}=\begin{cases}\overline{1}&\text{if }j\leq\mathfrak{d}_{1}-M+L\\
\overline{2}&\text{if }j>\mathfrak{d}_{1}-M+L\end{cases},

for some non-negative integers
L,M\leq\mathfrak{d}_{2}
.
Note that, for
m=r
, we have
\mathbf{3}=\overline{2}
and
\mathbf{2}=\overline{1}
.
Note also that solitons can not be longer than
n
in this case and the value of
n
may limit which of these highest weight states are possible.
When evolving the time of this genuine highest weight state,
we encounter cases that would not be covered by Lemma
B.4
.
So, the proof presented in Section
B.4
does not work for
m=r

(at least, not without major modifications).

However, Theorem
4.14
holds for the
m=r
case.

Example B.22
.

Consider a state composed of elements from the
U_{q}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(2|4))
crystal
B^{2,1}

(so that
m=r=2
).

\displaystyle t=0

4
3
1
2
1
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{1}
\overline{2}

\displaystyle t=1

4
3
1
2
1
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{1}
\overline{2}

\displaystyle t=2

4
3
1
2
1
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{1}
\overline{2}

\displaystyle t=3

4
1
3
2
1
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{1}
\overline{2}
\overline{2}

\displaystyle t=4

4
1
3
2
1
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{1}
\overline{2}
\overline{2}

\displaystyle t=5

4
1
3
2
1
\overline{2}
\overline{2}
\overline{1}
\overline{2}
\overline{2}

Note that this state satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
4.14

with
m=r
.
We observe that this state behaves solitonically.
Moreover
R({V_{\uparrow}}\otimes{W_{\uparrow}})=R(\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}&\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}&\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}&\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array})=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}&\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}&\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{2}$}&\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{1}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

and
R({V_{\downarrow}}\otimes{W_{\downarrow}})=R(\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr 1&3&4\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr 1&2\\
\hline\cr\end{array})=\begin{array}[]{|c|c|}\hline\cr 1&4\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr 1&2&3\\
\hline\cr\end{array}
.
The phase shift is
2=4-1-1=2\mathfrak{d}_{2}+H({V_{\uparrow}}\otimes{W_{\uparrow}})+H({V_{\downarrow}}\otimes{W_{\downarrow}})
.

We believe that it would be possible to prove this theorem by reducing it to a modified version of the
[
HI00
]
system by using something similar to an
L
-sped BBS. However, we would likely need to modify the speed of both solitons (as opposed to only of them in the
L
-sped BBS), which would make the proof much more techinical.
So, we instead prove the theorem by direct computation.

We first compute general results about the carrier,
similar to Lemma
B.4
.

Lemma B.23
.

Take some
\ell>0
.
Let
(\mathbf{x}_{j})_{j=1}^{b_{1}+a_{1}}
be a sequence
such that
1\leq\mathbf{x}_{1}<\mathbf{x}_{2}<\cdots<\mathbf{x}_{b_{2}+a_{1}}
.
Let
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}=\overline{2}^{a_{3}}\overline{1}^{a_{2}}(\mathbf{x}_{j})_{j=b_{1}+1}^{b_{1}+a_{1}}
for some
a_{3},a_{2},a_{1}
such that
a_{3}+a_{2}+a_{1}=\ell
.
Let
(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}=\overline{1}^{b_{2}}(\mathbf{x}_{j})_{j=1}^{b_{1}}(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=b_{2}+b_{1}+1}^{\ell}

for some
b_{2},b_{1}
such that
b_{2}+b_{1}\leq\ell
,
with
\mathbf{x}_{b_{1}}<\mathbf{z}_{b_{2}+b_{1}+1}<\cdots<\mathbf{z}_{\ell}
.

For

b=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{h}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=1}^{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{x}_{0}\\
\mathbf{y}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

with
1\leq\mathbf{x}_{0}<\mathbf{x}_{1}
and
\mathbf{x}_{b_{1}}\leq\mathbf{y}<\mathbf{z}_{b_{2}+b_{1}+1}
, we have that
R(b)=

(
n
a)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\overline{2}\\
\overline{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\
\overline{2}^{a_{3}-1}\overline{1}^{a_{2}}(\mathbf{x}_{j})_{j=b_{1}}^{b_{1}+a_{1}}\\
\overline{1}^{b_{2}-1}(\mathbf{x}_{j})_{j=0}^{b_{1}-1}\mathbf{y}(\mathbf{z}_{j})_{j=b_{2}+b_{1}+1}^{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

if
b_{2}>0
;

(
n
b)

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{h}_{a_{3}+a_{2}}\\
\mathbf{z}_{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\mathbf{X}^{\ell}\\

Lemma B.24
.

Assume
Q\leq M,L<\ell\leq d_{2}+1
.

Let
P=P_{1}\otimes P_{2}\otimes P_{3}\otimes\cdots
be the state

\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\
x_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\
x_{d_{1}-Q}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr x_{d_{1}-Q+1}\\
y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr x_{d_{1}}\\
y_{Q}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{1}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{1}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\
y_{L+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\
y_{d_{2}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty}

where

\displaystyle x_{j}=\begin{cases}d_{2}+d_{1}-M+1-j&\text{if}~j\leq d_{1}-M,\\
d_{1}+1-j&\text{if}~j>d_{1}-M,\end{cases},

\displaystyle y_{j}=d_{2}+1-j.

Let
\widetilde{C}=\min(d_{1},\ell)
. Then,

(i)

if

,

and

then

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr x_{d_{1}-Q}\\
y_{1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr x_{d_{1}}\\
y_{Q+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{1}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{1}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\
y_{L+1}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes u_{1}^{\otimes\infty};

and

then

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\cdots\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}\\
x_{d_{1}-Q}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\displaystyle\quad\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr x_{d_{1}-Q+1}\\
x_{d_{1}-M}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\otimes\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr x_{d_{1}-Q+2}\\

Proof.

Let
T_{\ell}(P)=\widetilde{P}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{2}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{3}\otimes\cdots

and let
u(j)
be the carrier at step
j
,
i.e.
R_{j-1}\cdots R_{1}R_{0}(P)=\widetilde{P}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes\widetilde{P}_{j}\otimes u(j)\otimes P_{j+1}\otimes\widetilde{P}_{j+2}\otimes\cdots
.
Let

u(j)=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}^{A_{3}(j)}\overline{1}^{A_{2}(j)}(\mathbf{x}^{j}_{k})_{k=B_{1}(j)+1}^{B_{1}(j)+A_{1}(j)}\\
\overline{1}^{B_{2}(j)}(\mathbf{x}^{j}_{k})_{k=1}^{B_{1}(j)}(\mathbf{z}^{j}_{k})_{k=B_{2}(j)+B_{1}(j)+1}^{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

for some sequences
(\mathbf{x}^{j}_{k})_{k=1}^{B_{1}(j)+A_{1}(j)}
and
(\mathbf{z}^{j}_{k})_{k=B_{2}(j)+B_{1}(j)+1}^{\ell}
such that
1\leq\mathbf{x}^{j}_{1}<\cdots<\mathbf{x}^{j}_{B_{1}(j)+A_{1}(j)}
and
1\leq\mathbf{z}^{j}_{B_{2}(j)+B_{1}(j)+1}<\cdots<\mathbf{z}^{j}_{\ell}

where
A_{3},A_{2},A_{1},B_{2},B_{1}
are functions on non-negative integers.
We will often write
A_{3}
for
A_{3}(j)
, with

being clear from context.
Similarly for the other functions.
Note that

u(0)=u_{\ell}=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}^{\ell}\\
\overline{1}^{\ell}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}.

Starting with
u(0)
, we can apply
(

¯
2

c)

\min(d_{1}-Q,\ell)
times:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\widetilde{P}_{\min(d_{1}-Q,\ell)}

\displaystyle=u_{1}^{\otimes\min(d_{1}-Q,\ell)}

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{|c|}\hline\cr\overline{2}^{\ell}\\

\hline\cr\end{array}\,.

(noting that
